Did you know they still don't really know how they built those things? Some people think it was aliens, which is crazy, but I watched a show on the History Channel once that said they used sound waves to levitate the blocks. Sound waves! It makes you wonder what kind of technology they had back then that we just forgot about. But yeah, people always say they can't keep up with my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think it's difficult for me to stay on topic, but other people sure seem to think so. Like I said, when I try to explain what is going on, my words run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm jumping all over the place. \n\nBut I'm just telling the story how it is. It's like when you're driving down the highway and you see a billboard for a diner, and then you start thinking about the best piece of cherry pie you ever had, which was at this little diner off Route 66 when I was a kid. My dad used to take us on these long road trips every summer. We had this old station wagon with the wood paneling on the side, you know the ones? The AC never worked right, so we'd have to roll the windows down and the wind would whip my hair all over my face. We'd always stop at these weird roadside attractions, like the world's biggest ball of twine or whatever. I always wanted to see the Grand Canyon, but we never made it that far west. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, staying on topic. Yeah, people tell me they get lost when I talk, but I think they just need to pay better attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or fragmented when you're not trying to explain them to others?\n\nPatient: No, not really. In my own head, everything lines up perfectly. It's just... well, like I keep saying, it's when I try to explain what is going on out loud. That's when my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's like they want everything in a straight line, but life isn't a straight line, you know? It's more like a spider web. Have you ever really looked at a spider web in the morning when the dew is on it? We get these huge garden spiders out by the chain-link fence at the warehouse. They're yellow and black, and they make these massive webs overnight. I tried to point one out to the new guy on the loading dock last week, but he was terrified of it. He wouldn't even go near the fence. I think people are too disconnected from nature these days. We spend all our time inside, breathing recycled air, staring at screens. My grandmother used to say that if you don't get your hands in the dirt at least once a week, your soul starts to dry up. She had this amazing tomato garden... \n\nBut yeah, in my head, it all makes sense. It's just other people who say I'm not making sense when I talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes I do. Like, I'll be in the aisle at work, or just folding clothes at home, and I hear this man's voice. Clear as day, he says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me. \n\nIt's so weird when it happens at home because I'll just be standing there with a basket of laundry. I hate folding fitted sheets, by the way. I don't think anyone actually knows how to fold them right. You always end up just rolling it into a ball and shoving it in the linen closet. My mom used to try and teach me how to tuck the corners into each other, but I never had the patience for it. She was always so particular about the laundry. Everything had to be ironed, even the pillowcases. Who irons pillowcases? But she had this old heavy iron, the kind you actually had to heat up on the stove. I think she got it at an antique store or a yard sale. Yard sales are great for finding stuff like that. I found this beautiful vintage mirror at one last summer, but the glass was a little warped, so when you look in it, you look kind of wavy, like you're underwater. \n\nBut yeah, I hear that guy's voice telling me to check things, even when I'm totally alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: It's just sometimes, you know? Like I was saying, it'll just happen when I'm in the aisle at work, or maybe when I'm folding clothes at home. I'll just hear this man's voice, clear as day, going, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me at all. \n\nIt's actually kind of startling when it happens at work because the warehouse is usually so loud. The forklifts are always beeping, and the big bay doors are rolling up and down. Those doors are so heavy, they sound like thunder when they shut. Last week, it was pouring rain, and every time the door opened, the wind would just whip the rain right inside. It made the concrete floor so slick. You have to be really careful on those floors when they're wet, or you'll go right down. My coworker, Dave, he slipped and almost took out a whole pallet of paper towels. Paper towels are surprisingly heavy when they're stacked that high. I always buy the cheap ones for my house, though. They don't absorb as well, but you go through them so fast anyway, what's the point of spending the extra money? \n\nBut yeah, it's not all the time. Just sometimes, out of nowhere, I'll hear him tell me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is related to the people at your workplace who you believe are watching you and changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I mean... it has to be, right? Because they are watching me. I know they are. I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my every move and changing the pallet counts just to mess with me, so I won't get that supervisor spot. \n\nIt's so obvious if you just look at how they act. The supervisor spot pays an extra three dollars an hour, which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up. You could buy a lot of groceries with that. Groceries are getting so expensive lately. I went to buy eggs the other day, and they were almost five dollars a carton. Five dollars! My aunt used to keep chickens in her backyard, Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They would lay these beautiful brown eggs. She would just go out every morning with a little wire basket and collect them. But then a fox got into the coop one night... it was a whole mess. Foxes are really clever, though. They look like dogs, but they move like cats. I saw one crossing the road late at night once, right near the highway exit. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice telling me to check things... it's because I have to. Because they're changing the counts on me to keep me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and changing the pallet counts are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have to be working together. Like I said, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's too coordinated for it just to be one person. \n\nThey all take their breaks at the exact same time, too. They always go out to the picnic tables behind the loading dock. Those tables are getting so worn out, the wood is all splintered. I got a splinter in my palm last month from resting my hand on one of them. I had to dig it out with a sewing needle. Needles always make me think of my grandmother. She was a seamstress, made all her own clothes. She had this old Singer sewing machine, the kind with the foot pedal you have to pump yourself. It was cast iron and had these beautiful gold painted flowers on it. She used to make me these little dresses with matching bloomers when I was a toddler. I still have one of them in a cedar chest at home. Cedar smells so good, doesn't it? It keeps the moths away. Moths are so gross when they fly in your face, all dusty and frantic. They always crowd around the porch light at night. \n\nBut yeah, they're definitely working together to mess with my counts and keep me from getting promoted.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me about a specific time when you saw someone changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to tell my husband about it, like when I see the forklift sheets don't match what I just counted. I try to explain what's going on, but my words just start running together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then I'll start thinking about the church signs down the street. They change the messages on those signs every week. Last week it said something about being a light in the dark, which is funny because our porch light burned out and my husband hasn't fixed it yet. His tone gets so sharp when I ask him to fix things. And when I try to explain how his tone connects to the forklift sheets and everything they're doing at work, people just look at me blankly. My husband, the people at work... they all tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm not making sense, but it makes perfect sense to me if they would just listen to how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people who are changing the pallet counts and watching you have a specific goal or plan in mind?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, their plan is to stop me from getting that supervisor spot. I told you, I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I look bad. \n\nAnd they want me to look bad because if I get that supervisor spot, I get a clipboard. The supervisors always carry these metal clipboards. They're so heavy, you could probably knock somebody out with one. I saw a movie once where a lady hit a burglar with a frying pan. It made this loud *clang* sound, like a bell ringing. Church bells ring every Sunday morning right down the street from my house. They start at 9 AM on the dot. It's so loud, it wakes up the neighbor's dog, and then he just barks and barks. He's a golden retriever, I think. Or maybe a yellow lab. They look so similar, don't they? Both of them love water. My cousin had a lab that would just jump right into the lake, even in November when the water was freezing. November is such a dreary month, all the leaves are gone and everything is just brown and gray... \n\nBut yeah, their plan is just to keep me down and keep me away from that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying a minute ago, yeah, I guess people think I do. When I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just kind of run together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then it just flows right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice. And people just stare at me and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nThey tell me I jump around too much. Jumping around... that makes me think of those little green frogs we used to catch in the creek behind my childhood home. We'd put them in this yellow plastic bucket. My brother left the bucket out in the sun once and they all dried up. It was awful. He was always doing mean stuff like that, though. He used to put rubber spiders in my work boots. I hate spiders, the way their legs move is just unnatural. Have you ever watched a spider spinning a web, though? It takes them hours, just going back and forth, back and forth. \n\nBut yeah, I guess my train of thought gets lost for other people, they just can't keep up with how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized, without any jumping around or difficulty explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes I think I'm being perfectly clear. But like I said, when I try to explain what's going on, my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone of voice. And people just tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nI don't get why it's so hard for them. My husband's tone of voice is just like his mother's. She used to call me every Sunday morning, right when those church bells started ringing. She'd talk for an hour about her garden. She grew these massive tomatoes, big as softballs. I tried growing tomatoes once, but the squirrels got to them before they even turned red. Squirrels are so fast, the way they twitch their tails and dart up the trees. There's a big oak tree in our front yard that drops acorns all over the driveway. I almost slipped on one the other day carrying groceries. We had a lot of groceries because they had a sale on canned soup. \n\nBut no, I guess I don't really have times where other people think my thoughts are clear. They just look at me like I'm not making sense, even when I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized without any difficulty explaining them to others, or is it always a struggle?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered this, doc. But no, it's pretty much always a struggle. Because like I keep trying to tell you, whenever I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just run together. I'll start talking about the forklift sheets, and then it goes right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice, and everyone just tells me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nAnd my husband's tone... he gets this really low, quiet voice when he's annoyed with me, like he thinks I can't hear him. But my hearing is actually really good. I can hear the fluorescent lights buzzing in the breakroom at the warehouse from all the way down the hall. They hum at this specific pitch, kind of like a bumblebee. I got stung by a bee when I was a kid, right on my eyelid. My whole face swelled up like a balloon. Have you ever seen those hot air balloons? I saw on TV once that they have a huge festival for them out in New Mexico or somewhere like that. Hundreds of them just floating up in the sky all at once, all those bright colors. I always wonder how they don't crash into each other up there...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your way of explaining things to others has always been like this, or is it something that has changed over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't always been like this. It's been going on for about 10 months now. It all started when the arguments with my husband got worse... right around the time I started trying for the shift-supervisor role at the warehouse. \n\nI really wanted that supervisor spot, you know? It came with a bump in pay, and they get to wear these bright orange vests instead of the regular yellow ones. Orange is such a loud color, though. It reminds me of those traffic cones they put out on the highway. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like years now. Every morning, it's just brake lights for miles. I sit in my car and listen to the radio, mostly the classic rock station. They play a lot of Led Zeppelin. My dad was a huge Zeppelin fan. He had this massive collection of vinyl records that he kept in milk crates in the basement. The basement always smelled like damp earth and old paper. I found a box of my old high school yearbooks down there once. I didn't even graduate, you know, I dropped out in the 11th grade. Sometimes I wonder if I should have stayed, but sitting in those classrooms under those fluorescent lights... just buzzing, buzzing, buzzing... it drove me crazy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Like I just said, no, it hasn't always been this way. It changed about 10 months ago. That's when the arguments with my husband started getting really bad, which was right around the time I was trying to get that shift-supervisor role.

Because he gets this real huffy tone with me, you know? \n\nIt sounds exactly like his uncle Earl. Earl used to run a little hardware store down on Main Street before they put in that big Home Depot and ran everybody out of business. Family doing that to family. He always wore these awful plaid shirts, too... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, people are always telling me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I jump around too much and they get lost when I go from the warehouse to the church to my husband. They just don't try hard enough to keep up, if you ask me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, to me they make perfect sense. It's all right there. But like I said, when I try to explain what is going on, my words just start running from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone of voice. And people just look at me blankly and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's really frustrating, honestly. It's like they're not even trying to listen. My husband is the worst about it. He just tunes me out completely. He does that thing where he just stares at the TV and nods, you know? He was watching this documentary about the pyramids last night. Did you know they still don't really know how they built those things? Some people think it was aliens, which is crazy, but I watched a show on the History Channel once that said they used sound waves to levitate the blocks. Sound waves! It makes you wonder what kind of technology they had back then that we just forgot about. But yeah, people always say they can't keep up with my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think it's difficult for me to stay on topic, but other people sure seem to think so. Like I said, when I try to explain what is going on, my words run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm jumping all over the place. \n\nBut I'm just telling the story how it is. It's like when you're driving down the highway and you see a billboard for a diner, and then you start thinking about the best piece of cherry pie you ever had, which was at this little diner off Route 66 when I was a kid. My dad used to take us on these long road trips every summer. We had this old station wagon with the wood paneling on the side, you know the ones? The AC never worked right, so we'd have to roll the windows down and the wind would whip my hair all over my face. We'd always stop at these weird roadside attractions, like the world's biggest ball of twine or whatever. I always wanted to see the Grand Canyon, but we never made it that far west. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, staying on topic. Yeah, people tell me they get lost when I talk, but I think they just need to pay better attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or fragmented when you're not trying to explain them to others?\n\nPatient: No, not really. In my own head, everything lines up perfectly. It's just... well, like I keep saying, it's when I try to explain what is going on out loud. That's when my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's like they want everything in a straight line, but life isn't a straight line, you know? It's more like a spider web. Have you ever really looked at a spider web in the morning when the dew is on it? We get these huge garden spiders out by the chain-link fence at the warehouse. They're yellow and black, and they make these massive webs overnight. I tried to point one out to the new guy on the loading dock last week, but he was terrified of it. He wouldn't even go near the fence. I think people are too disconnected from nature these days. We spend all our time inside, breathing recycled air, staring at screens. My grandmother used to say that if you don't get your hands in the dirt at least once a week, your soul starts to dry up. She had this amazing tomato garden... \n\nBut yeah, in my head, it all makes sense. It's just other people who say I'm not making sense when I talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes I do. Like, I'll be in the aisle at work, or just folding clothes at home, and I hear this man's voice. Clear as day, he says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me. \n\nIt's so weird when it happens at home because I'll just be standing there with a basket of laundry. I hate folding fitted sheets, by the way. I don't think anyone actually knows how to fold them right. You always end up just rolling it into a ball and shoving it in the linen closet. My mom used to try and teach me how to tuck the corners into each other, but I never had the patience for it. She was always so particular about the laundry. Everything had to be ironed, even the pillowcases. Who irons pillowcases? But she had this old heavy iron, the kind you actually had to heat up on the stove. I think she got it at an antique store or a yard sale. Yard sales are great for finding stuff like that. I found this beautiful vintage mirror at one last summer, but the glass was a little warped, so when you look in it, you look kind of wavy, like you're underwater. \n\nBut yeah, I hear that guy's voice telling me to check things, even when I'm totally alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: It's just sometimes, you know? Like I was saying, it'll just happen when I'm in the aisle at work, or maybe when I'm folding clothes at home. I'll just hear this man's voice, clear as day, going, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me at all. \n\nIt's actually kind of startling when it happens at work because the warehouse is usually so loud. The forklifts are always beeping, and the big bay doors are rolling up and down. Those doors are so heavy, they sound like thunder when they shut. Last week, it was pouring rain, and every time the door opened, the wind would just whip the rain right inside. It made the concrete floor so slick. You have to be really careful on those floors when they're wet, or you'll go right down. My coworker, Dave, he slipped and almost took out a whole pallet of paper towels. Paper towels are surprisingly heavy when they're stacked that high. I always buy the cheap ones for my house, though. They don't absorb as well, but you go through them so fast anyway, what's the point of spending the extra money? \n\nBut yeah, it's not all the time. Just sometimes, out of nowhere, I'll hear him tell me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is related to the people at your workplace who you believe are watching you and changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I mean... it has to be, right? Because they are watching me. I know they are. I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my every move and changing the pallet counts just to mess with me, so I won't get that supervisor spot. \n\nIt's so obvious if you just look at how they act. The supervisor spot pays an extra three dollars an hour, which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up. You could buy a lot of groceries with that. Groceries are getting so expensive lately. I went to buy eggs the other day, and they were almost five dollars a carton. Five dollars! My aunt used to keep chickens in her backyard, Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They would lay these beautiful brown eggs. She would just go out every morning with a little wire basket and collect them. But then a fox got into the coop one night... it was a whole mess. Foxes are really clever, though. They look like dogs, but they move like cats. I saw one crossing the road late at night once, right near the highway exit. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice telling me to check things... it's because I have to. Because they're changing the counts on me to keep me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and changing the pallet counts are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have to be working together. Like I said, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's too coordinated for it just to be one person. \n\nThey all take their breaks at the exact same time, too. They always go out to the picnic tables behind the loading dock. Those tables are getting so worn out, the wood is all splintered. I got a splinter in my palm last month from resting my hand on one of them. I had to dig it out with a sewing needle. Needles always make me think of my grandmother. She was a seamstress, made all her own clothes. She had this old Singer sewing machine, the kind with the foot pedal you have to pump yourself. It was cast iron and had these beautiful gold painted flowers on it. She used to make me these little dresses with matching bloomers when I was a toddler. I still have one of them in a cedar chest at home. Cedar smells so good, doesn't it? It keeps the moths away. Moths are so gross when they fly in your face, all dusty and frantic. They always crowd around the porch light at night. \n\nBut yeah, they're definitely working together to mess with my counts and keep me from getting promoted.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me about a specific time when you saw someone changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to tell my husband about it, like when I see the forklift sheets don't match what I just counted. I try to explain what's going on, but my words just start running together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then I'll start thinking about the church signs down the street. They change the messages on those signs every week. Last week it said something about being a light in the dark, which is funny because our porch light burned out and my husband hasn't fixed it yet. His tone gets so sharp when I ask him to fix things. And when I try to explain how his tone connects to the forklift sheets and everything they're doing at work, people just look at me blankly. My husband, the people at work... they all tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm not making sense, but it makes perfect sense to me if they would just listen to how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people who are changing the pallet counts and watching you have a specific goal or plan in mind?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, their plan is to stop me from getting that supervisor spot. I told you, I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I look bad. \n\nAnd they want me to look bad because if I get that supervisor spot, I get a clipboard. The supervisors always carry these metal clipboards. They're so heavy, you could probably knock somebody out with one. I saw a movie once where a lady hit a burglar with a frying pan. It made this loud *clang* sound, like a bell ringing. Church bells ring every Sunday morning right down the street from my house. They start at 9 AM on the dot. It's so loud, it wakes up the neighbor's dog, and then he just barks and barks. He's a golden retriever, I think. Or maybe a yellow lab. They look so similar, don't they? Both of them love water. My cousin had a lab that would just jump right into the lake, even in November when the water was freezing. November is such a dreary month, all the leaves are gone and everything is just brown and gray... \n\nBut yeah, their plan is just to keep me down and keep me away from that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying a minute ago, yeah, I guess people think I do. When I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just kind of run together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then it just flows right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice. And people just stare at me and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nThey tell me I jump around too much. Jumping around... that makes me think of those little green frogs we used to catch in the creek behind my childhood home. We'd put them in this yellow plastic bucket. My brother left the bucket out in the sun once and they all dried up. It was awful. He was always doing mean stuff like that, though. He used to put rubber spiders in my work boots. I hate spiders, the way their legs move is just unnatural. Have you ever watched a spider spinning a web, though? It takes them hours, just going back and forth, back and forth. \n\nBut yeah, I guess my train of thought gets lost for other people, they just can't keep up with how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized, without any jumping around or difficulty explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes I think I'm being perfectly clear. But like I said, when I try to explain what's going on, my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone of voice. And people just tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nI don't get why it's so hard for them. My husband's tone of voice is just like his mother's. She used to call me every Sunday morning, right when those church bells started ringing. She'd talk for an hour about her garden. She grew these massive tomatoes, big as softballs. I tried growing tomatoes once, but the squirrels got to them before they even turned red. Squirrels are so fast, the way they twitch their tails and dart up the trees. There's a big oak tree in our front yard that drops acorns all over the driveway. I almost slipped on one the other day carrying groceries. We had a lot of groceries because they had a sale on canned soup. \n\nBut no, I guess I don't really have times where other people think my thoughts are clear. They just look at me like I'm not making sense, even when I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized without any difficulty explaining them to others, or is it always a struggle?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered this, doc. But no, it's pretty much always a struggle. Because like I keep trying to tell you, whenever I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just run together. I'll start talking about the forklift sheets, and then it goes right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice, and everyone just tells me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nAnd my husband's tone... he gets this really low, quiet voice when he's annoyed with me, like he thinks I can't hear him. But my hearing is actually really good. I can hear the fluorescent lights buzzing in the breakroom at the warehouse from all the way down the hall. They hum at this specific pitch, kind of like a bumblebee. I got stung by a bee when I was a kid, right on my eyelid. My whole face swelled up like a balloon. Have you ever seen those hot air balloons? I saw on TV once that they have a huge festival for them out in New Mexico or somewhere like that. Hundreds of them just floating up in the sky all at once, all those bright colors. I always wonder how they don't crash into each other up there...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your way of explaining things to others has always been like this, or is it something that has changed over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't always been like this. It's been going on for about 10 months now. It all started when the arguments with my husband got worse... right around the time I started trying for the shift-supervisor role at the warehouse. \n\nI really wanted that supervisor spot, you know? It came with a bump in pay, and they get to wear these bright orange vests instead of the regular yellow ones. Orange is such a loud color, though. It reminds me of those traffic cones they put out on the highway. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like years now. Every morning, it's just brake lights for miles. I sit in my car and listen to the radio, mostly the classic rock station. They play a lot of Led Zeppelin. My dad was a huge Zeppelin fan. He had this massive collection of vinyl records that he kept in milk crates in the basement. The basement always smelled like damp earth and old paper. I found a box of my old high school yearbooks down there once. I didn't even graduate, you know, I dropped out in the 11th grade. Sometimes I wonder if I should have stayed, but sitting in those classrooms under those fluorescent lights... just buzzing, buzzing, buzzing... it drove me crazy.\n\nDoctor: Was your way of thinking and explaining things always like this, or did it change at some point?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Like I just said, no, it hasn't always been this way. It changed about 10 months ago. That's when the arguments with my husband started getting really bad, which was right around the time I was trying to get that shift-supervisor role. \n\nI actually had to sit down and do a real interview for it with the floor manager, Mr. Henderson. He always wears these super stiff button-down shirts. You know the kind that look like they just came straight from the dry cleaners in that plastic wrap? I can't stand the smell of dry cleaning, it smells like harsh chemicals. It reminds me of the chlorine at the public pool. We used to go to the municipal pool all the time when I was little, just swimming for hours until our fingers got all wrinkly like little raisins. And then the ice cream truck would come by playing that same song over and over. I think it was 'Turkey in the Straw' or something like that. It just loops and loops. Have you ever noticed how ice cream trucks always have the creepiest sounding music when the speakers start going bad? It gets all warped and slow. My husband says I eat too much ice cream anyway, but he's the one buying the big tubs of rocky road every time he goes to the grocery store.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Are you... are you messing with me, doc? I feel like I'm in the Twilight Zone or something. You just asked me that. Twice. 

But no, I don't have times where it's easy. It's always a mess. Like I said, when I try to explain what's going on, my words just start running together. Family doing that to family. He always wore these awful plaid shirts, too... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, people are always telling me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I jump around too much and they get lost when I go from the warehouse to the church to my husband. They just don't try hard enough to keep up, if you ask me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: I mean, to me they make perfect sense. It's all right there. But like I said, when I try to explain what is going on, my words just start running from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone of voice. And people just look at me blankly and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's really frustrating, honestly. It's like they're not even trying to listen. My husband is the worst about it. He just tunes me out completely. He does that thing where he just stares at the TV and nods, you know? He was watching this documentary about the pyramids last night. Did you know they still don't really know how they built those things? Some people think it was aliens, which is crazy, but I watched a show on the History Channel once that said they used sound waves to levitate the blocks. Sound waves! It makes you wonder what kind of technology they had back then that we just forgot about. But yeah, people always say they can't keep up with my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think it's difficult for me to stay on topic, but other people sure seem to think so. Like I said, when I try to explain what is going on, my words run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm jumping all over the place. \n\nBut I'm just telling the story how it is. It's like when you're driving down the highway and you see a billboard for a diner, and then you start thinking about the best piece of cherry pie you ever had, which was at this little diner off Route 66 when I was a kid. My dad used to take us on these long road trips every summer. We had this old station wagon with the wood paneling on the side, you know the ones? The AC never worked right, so we'd have to roll the windows down and the wind would whip my hair all over my face. We'd always stop at these weird roadside attractions, like the world's biggest ball of twine or whatever. I always wanted to see the Grand Canyon, but we never made it that far west. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, staying on topic. Yeah, people tell me they get lost when I talk, but I think they just need to pay better attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or fragmented when you're not trying to explain them to others?\n\nPatient: No, not really. In my own head, everything lines up perfectly. It's just... well, like I keep saying, it's when I try to explain what is going on out loud. That's when my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone, and people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's like they want everything in a straight line, but life isn't a straight line, you know? It's more like a spider web. Have you ever really looked at a spider web in the morning when the dew is on it? We get these huge garden spiders out by the chain-link fence at the warehouse. They're yellow and black, and they make these massive webs overnight. I tried to point one out to the new guy on the loading dock last week, but he was terrified of it. He wouldn't even go near the fence. I think people are too disconnected from nature these days. We spend all our time inside, breathing recycled air, staring at screens. My grandmother used to say that if you don't get your hands in the dirt at least once a week, your soul starts to dry up. She had this amazing tomato garden... \n\nBut yeah, in my head, it all makes sense. It's just other people who say I'm not making sense when I talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes I do. Like, I'll be in the aisle at work, or just folding clothes at home, and I hear this man's voice. Clear as day, he says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me. \n\nIt's so weird when it happens at home because I'll just be standing there with a basket of laundry. I hate folding fitted sheets, by the way. I don't think anyone actually knows how to fold them right. You always end up just rolling it into a ball and shoving it in the linen closet. My mom used to try and teach me how to tuck the corners into each other, but I never had the patience for it. She was always so particular about the laundry. Everything had to be ironed, even the pillowcases. Who irons pillowcases? But she had this old heavy iron, the kind you actually had to heat up on the stove. I think she got it at an antique store or a yard sale. Yard sales are great for finding stuff like that. I found this beautiful vintage mirror at one last summer, but the glass was a little warped, so when you look in it, you look kind of wavy, like you're underwater. \n\nBut yeah, I hear that guy's voice telling me to check things, even when I'm totally alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: It's just sometimes, you know? Like I was saying, it'll just happen when I'm in the aisle at work, or maybe when I'm folding clothes at home. I'll just hear this man's voice, clear as day, going, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody near me at all. \n\nIt's actually kind of startling when it happens at work because the warehouse is usually so loud. The forklifts are always beeping, and the big bay doors are rolling up and down. Those doors are so heavy, they sound like thunder when they shut. Last week, it was pouring rain, and every time the door opened, the wind would just whip the rain right inside. It made the concrete floor so slick. You have to be really careful on those floors when they're wet, or you'll go right down. My coworker, Dave, he slipped and almost took out a whole pallet of paper towels. Paper towels are surprisingly heavy when they're stacked that high. I always buy the cheap ones for my house, though. They don't absorb as well, but you go through them so fast anyway, what's the point of spending the extra money? \n\nBut yeah, it's not all the time. Just sometimes, out of nowhere, I'll hear him tell me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is related to the people at your workplace who you believe are watching you and changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I mean... it has to be, right? Because they are watching me. I know they are. I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my every move and changing the pallet counts just to mess with me, so I won't get that supervisor spot. \n\nIt's so obvious if you just look at how they act. The supervisor spot pays an extra three dollars an hour, which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up. You could buy a lot of groceries with that. Groceries are getting so expensive lately. I went to buy eggs the other day, and they were almost five dollars a carton. Five dollars! My aunt used to keep chickens in her backyard, Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They would lay these beautiful brown eggs. She would just go out every morning with a little wire basket and collect them. But then a fox got into the coop one night... it was a whole mess. Foxes are really clever, though. They look like dogs, but they move like cats. I saw one crossing the road late at night once, right near the highway exit. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice telling me to check things... it's because I have to. Because they're changing the counts on me to keep me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and changing the pallet counts are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have to be working together. Like I said, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's too coordinated for it just to be one person. \n\nThey all take their breaks at the exact same time, too. They always go out to the picnic tables behind the loading dock. Those tables are getting so worn out, the wood is all splintered. I got a splinter in my palm last month from resting my hand on one of them. I had to dig it out with a sewing needle. Needles always make me think of my grandmother. She was a seamstress, made all her own clothes. She had this old Singer sewing machine, the kind with the foot pedal you have to pump yourself. It was cast iron and had these beautiful gold painted flowers on it. She used to make me these little dresses with matching bloomers when I was a toddler. I still have one of them in a cedar chest at home. Cedar smells so good, doesn't it? It keeps the moths away. Moths are so gross when they fly in your face, all dusty and frantic. They always crowd around the porch light at night. \n\nBut yeah, they're definitely working together to mess with my counts and keep me from getting promoted.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me about a specific time when you saw someone changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to tell my husband about it, like when I see the forklift sheets don't match what I just counted. I try to explain what's going on, but my words just start running together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then I'll start thinking about the church signs down the street. They change the messages on those signs every week. Last week it said something about being a light in the dark, which is funny because our porch light burned out and my husband hasn't fixed it yet. His tone gets so sharp when I ask him to fix things. And when I try to explain how his tone connects to the forklift sheets and everything they're doing at work, people just look at me blankly. My husband, the people at work... they all tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm not making sense, but it makes perfect sense to me if they would just listen to how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people who are changing the pallet counts and watching you have a specific goal or plan in mind?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, their plan is to stop me from getting that supervisor spot. I told you, I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I look bad. \n\nAnd they want me to look bad because if I get that supervisor spot, I get a clipboard. The supervisors always carry these metal clipboards. They're so heavy, you could probably knock somebody out with one. I saw a movie once where a lady hit a burglar with a frying pan. It made this loud *clang* sound, like a bell ringing. Church bells ring every Sunday morning right down the street from my house. They start at 9 AM on the dot. It's so loud, it wakes up the neighbor's dog, and then he just barks and barks. He's a golden retriever, I think. Or maybe a yellow lab. They look so similar, don't they? Both of them love water. My cousin had a lab that would just jump right into the lake, even in November when the water was freezing. November is such a dreary month, all the leaves are gone and everything is just brown and gray... \n\nBut yeah, their plan is just to keep me down and keep me away from that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying a minute ago, yeah, I guess people think I do. When I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just kind of run together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then it just flows right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice. And people just stare at me and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nThey tell me I jump around too much. Jumping around... that makes me think of those little green frogs we used to catch in the creek behind my childhood home. We'd put them in this yellow plastic bucket. My brother left the bucket out in the sun once and they all dried up. It was awful. He was always doing mean stuff like that, though. He used to put rubber spiders in my work boots. I hate spiders, the way their legs move is just unnatural. Have you ever watched a spider spinning a web, though? It takes them hours, just going back and forth, back and forth. \n\nBut yeah, I guess my train of thought gets lost for other people, they just can't keep up with how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized, without any jumping around or difficulty explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes I think I'm being perfectly clear. I tried growing tomatoes once, but the squirrels got to them before they even turned red. Squirrels are so fast, the way they twitch their tails and dart up the trees. We had a lot of groceries because they had a sale on canned soup. \n\nBut no, I guess I don't really have times where other people think my thoughts are clear. They just look at me like I'm not making sense, even when I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized without any difficulty explaining them to others, or is it always a struggle?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered this, doc. Hundreds of them just floating up in the sky all at once, all those bright colors. It reminds me of those traffic cones they put out on the highway. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like years now. Every morning, it's just brake lights for miles. I sit in my car and listen to the radio, mostly the classic rock station. They play a lot of Led Zeppelin. My dad was a huge Zeppelin fan. He had this massive collection of vinyl records that he kept in milk crates in the basement. The basement always smelled like damp earth and old paper. I can't stand the smell of dry cleaning, it smells like harsh chemicals. It reminds me of the chlorine at the public pool. We used to go to the municipal pool all the time when I was little, just swimming for hours until our fingers got all wrinkly like little raisins. And then the ice cream truck would come by playing that same song over and over. I think it was 'Turkey in the Straw' or something like that. It just loops and loops. Foxes are really clever, though. They look like dogs, but they move like cats. I saw one crossing the road late at night once, right near the highway exit. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice telling me to check things... it's because I have to. Because they're changing the counts on me to keep me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and changing the pallet counts are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have to be working together. Like I said, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's too coordinated for it just to be one person. \n\nThey all take their breaks at the exact same time, too. They always go out to the picnic tables behind the loading dock. Those tables are getting so worn out, the wood is all splintered. I got a splinter in my palm last month from resting my hand on one of them. I had to dig it out with a sewing needle. Needles always make me think of my grandmother. She was a seamstress, made all her own clothes. She had this old Singer sewing machine, the kind with the foot pedal you have to pump yourself. It was cast iron and had these beautiful gold painted flowers on it. She used to make me these little dresses with matching bloomers when I was a toddler. I still have one of them in a cedar chest at home. Cedar smells so good, doesn't it? It keeps the moths away. Moths are so gross when they fly in your face, all dusty and frantic. They always crowd around the porch light at night. \n\nBut yeah, they're definitely working together to mess with my counts and keep me from getting promoted.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me about a specific time when you saw someone changing the pallet counts?\n\nPatient: Well, I try to tell my husband about it, like when I see the forklift sheets don't match what I just counted. I try to explain what's going on, but my words just start running together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then I'll start thinking about the church signs down the street. They change the messages on those signs every week. Last week it said something about being a light in the dark, which is funny because our porch light burned out and my husband hasn't fixed it yet. His tone gets so sharp when I ask him to fix things. And when I try to explain how his tone connects to the forklift sheets and everything they're doing at work, people just look at me blankly. My husband, the people at work... they all tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. They say I'm not making sense, but it makes perfect sense to me if they would just listen to how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people who are changing the pallet counts and watching you have a specific goal or plan in mind?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, their plan is to stop me from getting that supervisor spot. I told you, I came in here because my husband and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know what's going on. They've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I look bad. \n\nAnd they want me to look bad because if I get that supervisor spot, I get a clipboard. The supervisors always carry these metal clipboards. They're so heavy, you could probably knock somebody out with one. I saw a movie once where a lady hit a burglar with a frying pan. It made this loud *clang* sound, like a bell ringing. Church bells ring every Sunday morning right down the street from my house. They start at 9 AM on the dot. It's so loud, it wakes up the neighbor's dog, and then he just barks and barks. He's a golden retriever, I think. Or maybe a yellow lab. They look so similar, don't they? Both of them love water. My cousin had a lab that would just jump right into the lake, even in November when the water was freezing. November is such a dreary month, all the leaves are gone and everything is just brown and gray... \n\nBut yeah, their plan is just to keep me down and keep me away from that promotion.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying a minute ago, yeah, I guess people think I do. When I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just kind of run together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets, and then it just flows right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice. And people just stare at me and tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nThey tell me I jump around too much. Jumping around... that makes me think of those little green frogs we used to catch in the creek behind my childhood home. We'd put them in this yellow plastic bucket. My brother left the bucket out in the sun once and they all dried up. It was awful. He was always doing mean stuff like that, though. He used to put rubber spiders in my work boots. I hate spiders, the way their legs move is just unnatural. Have you ever watched a spider spinning a web, though? It takes them hours, just going back and forth, back and forth. \n\nBut yeah, I guess my train of thought gets lost for other people, they just can't keep up with how it all connects.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized, without any jumping around or difficulty explaining them to others?\n\nPatient: Well, sometimes I think I'm being perfectly clear. But like I said, when I try to explain what's going on, my words just run from the forklift sheets to the church signs to my husband's tone of voice. And people just tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nI don't get why it's so hard for them. My husband's tone of voice is just like his mother's. She used to call me every Sunday morning, right when those church bells started ringing. She'd talk for an hour about her garden. She grew these massive tomatoes, big as softballs. I tried growing tomatoes once, but the squirrels got to them before they even turned red. Squirrels are so fast, the way they twitch their tails and dart up the trees. There's a big oak tree in our front yard that drops acorns all over the driveway. I almost slipped on one the other day carrying groceries. We had a lot of groceries because they had a sale on canned soup. \n\nBut no, I guess I don't really have times where other people think my thoughts are clear. They just look at me like I'm not making sense, even when I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized without any difficulty explaining them to others, or is it always a struggle?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered this, doc. But no, it's pretty much always a struggle. Because like I keep trying to tell you, whenever I try to explain what's actually going on, my words just run together. I'll start talking about the forklift sheets, and then it goes right into the church signs, and then my husband's tone of voice, and everyone just tells me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nAnd my husband's tone... he gets this really low, quiet voice when he's annoyed with me, like he thinks I can't hear him. But my hearing is actually really good. I can hear the fluorescent lights buzzing in the breakroom at the warehouse from all the way down the hall. They hum at this specific pitch, kind of like a bumblebee. I got stung by a bee when I was a kid, right on my eyelid. My whole face swelled up like a balloon. Have you ever seen those hot air balloons? I saw on TV once that they have a huge festival for them out in New Mexico or somewhere like that. Hundreds of them just floating up in the sky all at once, all those bright colors. I always wonder how they don't crash into each other up there...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your way of explaining things to others has always been like this, or is it something that has changed over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it hasn't always been like this. It's been going on for about 10 months now. It all started when the arguments with my husband got worse... right around the time I started trying for the shift-supervisor role at the warehouse. \n\nI really wanted that supervisor spot, you know? It came with a bump in pay, and they get to wear these bright orange vests instead of the regular yellow ones. Orange is such a loud color, though. It reminds me of those traffic cones they put out on the highway. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like years now. Every morning, it's just brake lights for miles. I sit in my car and listen to the radio, mostly the classic rock station. They play a lot of Led Zeppelin. My dad was a huge Zeppelin fan. He had this massive collection of vinyl records that he kept in milk crates in the basement. The basement always smelled like damp earth and old paper. I found a box of my old high school yearbooks down there once. I didn't even graduate, you know, I dropped out in the 11th grade. Sometimes I wonder if I should have stayed, but sitting in those classrooms under those fluorescent lights... just buzzing, buzzing, buzzing... it drove me crazy.\n\nDoctor: Was your way of thinking and explaining things always like this, or did it change at some point?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Like I just said, no, it hasn't always been this way. It changed about 10 months ago. That's when the arguments with my husband started getting really bad, which was right around the time I was trying to get that shift-supervisor role. \n\nI actually had to sit down and do a real interview for it with the floor manager, Mr. Henderson. He always wears these super stiff button-down shirts. You know the kind that look like they just came straight from the dry cleaners in that plastic wrap? I can't stand the smell of dry cleaning, it smells like harsh chemicals. It reminds me of the chlorine at the public pool. We used to go to the municipal pool all the time when I was little, just swimming for hours until our fingers got all wrinkly like little raisins. And then the ice cream truck would come by playing that same song over and over. I think it was 'Turkey in the Straw' or something like that. It just loops and loops. Have you ever noticed how ice cream trucks always have the creepiest sounding music when the speakers start going bad? It gets all warped and slow. My husband says I eat too much ice cream anyway, but he's the one buying the big tubs of rocky road every time he goes to the grocery store.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts are completely organized and easy to explain to others, without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: Are you... are you messing with me, doc? I feel like I'm in the Twilight Zone or something. You just asked me that. Twice. \n\nBut no, I don't have times where it's easy. It's always a mess. Like I said, when I try to explain what's going on, my words just start running together. I'll be talking about the forklift sheets at work, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church signs down the street, and then I'm talking about how my husband's tone of voice sounds, and everyone just looks at me like I have two heads. They always tell me they can't follow what I'm saying. \n\nIt's really frustrating, honestly. Almost as frustrating as trying to get the shrink wrap off the pallets at work. They wrap those things so tight, you have to use a box cutter to get through it. I lost my favorite box cutter last week. It had a bright green handle. Green is my favorite color. It reminds me of the grass in the spring right after it rains. You know that smell? Petrichor, I think it's called. I read that in a book once. I don't read much anymore, though.
